2. MASTER’S INSTRUCTION
2.1. General 
(1) This booklet is m
ade for th e guidance to the master so as to have the safe operation and
proper loading
of the vessel.
(2) This booklet present the necessary data and instructions to arrange cargoes, fuel oil,
ballast water etc. in such a way that the vessel can be free from creation of unacceptable
stress in hull structure and maintain suffic ient stability under each loading condition
during service.
(3) The master of the vessel shoul d read the whole of the booklet  and be well familiar with
the characteristic of the vessel before placing it into service. Any ignorance of the
instructions in this booklet may jeopardize the safety of crew and vessel.
(4) It should be noted that however this booklet can not cover unusual condition or
contingencies.
(5) Calculation in the booklet is obtained from NAPA program of NAPA Oy. Helsinki.
Finland. Napa Release 2006.1.
(6) Coordinate system in the booklet is right-h anded. Origin of the coordinate system is
located at the intersection of frame0 (AP), centerline of ship (CL) and the base line (BL).
Positive value (+) Negative value (-) Remark

2.2. S
tability Guidance 
The l
oading conditions given in this booklet are more or less fictitious, just to illustrate the features of the 
vessel representing the whole range of operation. Of co urse they comply with regulations are required for 
the ship. In general those are: 
—  Freeboard (see chapter ‘1.6 Freeboard Mark and Deadweight Scale’) 
—  Longitudinal strength (see chapter ‘2 .4 Loading Criteria and Instruction’) 
—  Intact and damage stability criteria (see chapter ‘3. Stability Data’) 
An existing loading condition that differs from those in this booklet should be investigated whether these 
criterions are met. This can be done with the aid of loading computer easily and get reliable results. 
However if the computer fails or what ever happens that precludes usage of which, then the following is  
optional:  
—  Checking of freeboard can be done by reading of draft marks; 
—  Longitudinal strength and immersion anger of openings can not be determined with aid of this 
manual;  
—  for checking stability there are two possibilities: The easiest way is to determine the center of gravity, 
correcting it by the effect of free surface if any and see whether this value is in stable zone of 
diagrams in chapter ‘3.6 Stability Limiting Curve’. This diagram contains of intact, damage and wind 
age stability. Alternatively-in doubt or whether otherwise required-a curve of righting arms can be 
calculated and drawn to check in tact stability criteria(see chap ter ‘2.5.2 Trim and Stability 
Calculation’). Damage stability can be checked via required KG
mi
n val
ues(see chapter  ‘3.6 Stability 
Limiting Curve’). 

===== PDF页 12 =====
共340页 D

第 12 页
2.3. General Pr
ecautions against Capsizing 
(1) Com
pliance with the stability criteria indicated hereinafter does not ensure immunity against capsizing
regardless of the circumstance or preventing the master from his responsibility. Master should
therefore have prudence and good seamanship in the season of the year, weather forecast and the 
navigational zone and take the appropriate action to speed and course according to prevailing 
circumstances as well as ICLL66and Protocol of1988 for damage stability. 
(2) Master should ensure that the cargo stowed in the ship is compliant with the criteria. If necessary the
weight of cargo should be limited to the extend that ballast weight may be required.
(3) Before a voyage care Master should ensure that  the cargo and sizeable pieces of equipment have been
properly loaded to minimize the possibility of both longitudinal and lateral shifting while at sea, on the
effect of acceleration caused by rolling and pitching.
(4) In all loading conditions, the initial metacentric hei ght should be corrected for the free surface of each
kind of tank which having larger free surface, and selecting the maxim free surface moment of these
tanks. The correction of stability curves by the method of increasing the gravity center of ship, the
increasing value is the corrective value for sum of free surface of tanks.
(5) Weather tight closing devices such as doors, hatches, etc. should be kept close during navigation. No
modification or removal is allowed for doorsills, hatch comings, air piping, ventilators and other piping
systems without authorization by the classification society.
(6) Master should note that stability can be adversely affected by such influence as beam wind on ships
with large wind age area, water trapped on deck and in deck cargo, rolling characteristics and following
seas.
(7) Master should be aware of the possibility that the carriage of certain bulk cargo may adversely affect
the stability of the ship. Attention is drawn to the recommendations made in the IMO’s code of safe
practice for solid bulk cargo.

2.4. Loading Criter
ia and Instruction 
The sta
ndard loading conditions described in this booklet fully warrant all of trim, stability and 
longitudinal strength. In case to make the loading condition different from which in this booklet, the master 
should comply with the criteria of stability and longitudinal strength in any condition. It is recommended 
to comply with the requirement as follows: 
(1) Designed allowable local loading on inner bottom
Cargo hold 1 2 3 4 5 
Max
imum uniform loading(t/m2) 
22 22 22 22 22 
(2)
Stability
It is necessary to comply with the requirement of stability criteria of IMO document about intact
stability shown in ‘2.6 Stability Criteria Evaluation’
. T
o keep necessary righting lever and metacentric
height, care should be taken on the following points.
a) Cargo weight distribution in vertical direction.
b) Filling up proper ballast water in tanks.
c) Minimizing free surface due to ballast water and oil.
In each loading condition, GM value due to free surface of liquids in tanks should be higher than GM 
limit curve obtained from the intact stability criteria. Besides intact stability, keeping the vessel at a 
satisfactory safety level in view of damage stability, the GM value should also be higher than GM limit 
curve obtained from the damage stability criteria. The two GM limit curves are shown in “3.6 Stability 
Li
miting Curves”. 
(3) Da
mage stability for SOLAS74 Chapter XII
Additional safety measures for bulk carriers of “Amendments to the annex to the International
Convention for the Safety of Life at Sea.1974”.
The following criteria in condition of equilibrium after flooding have been considered:
1) The final waterline after flooding, taking into account signage, heel and trim is below the lower edge
of any opening through flooding may take place.
2) The angle of heel due to unsymmetrical flooding does not exceed 15
°. If 
no part of the deck is
immersed an angle of heel up to 17° m
ay be accepted.
3) The metacentric height in the flooded condition is positive.
4) When any part of the deck outside the compartment assumed flooded in a particular case of damage is
immersed or in any case where the margin of stability in the flooded condition may be considered, the
residual stability is to be investigated:
a) The righting lever curve should have a minimum range of 20
° b
eyond the position of equilibrium
with a maximum righting lever of at least 0.1m within this range.
b) The area under the righting lever curve within this range should not be less than 0.0175mrad.

(4) Longitudinal s
trength
The scantlings are suitable for maximum permissible still water bending moment(BM) of 1,070,000
KN·m and -950,000KN·m amid ship in sea-going conditions. And the scantlings are suitable for a
maximum permissible bending moment(BM) of 1,360,000 KN·m and -1,310,000 KN·m amid ship in
flooded conditions.
In any loading condition and flooded condition the BM and SF value should not be more than the 
maximum permissible values. The details of designed permissible SWBM and SWSF are as follows:

(5) Local strength
Based
 on the design loading criteria for local strength, hold mass curve via maximum allowable and
minimum required mass of cargo and double bottom capability of each hold as a function of the
draught at mid-hold position are shown. In any loading condition, mass of cargo in each hold should be
in the allowable area of the hold mass curve.
(6) Minimum draft forward
The scantling is approved for operation at draft forward not less than 3.8 meters. In the opinion of the
master, sea conditions are likely to cause regular slamming; other appropriation measures such as a
change in speed, heeling or an increase in the draught forward may also need to be taken.
(7) Bridge visibility
The view of the sea surface from the conning position in wheel house should not be obscured more
than two shop lengths, forward of the bow to 10° o
n each side irrespective of the ship’s draft trim.
(8) Propeller size, location and immersion
Propeller diameter D = 5.3m;
Height of the shaft line from the base line = 3.2m;
To keep efficiency of propeller, draft aft should be deep enough to take sufficient propeller immersion.
For the heavy ballast condition the propeller immersion I/D is to be at least 60% where:
I: the distance from propeller centerline to waterline 
 D: propeller diameter.

2.5. Calculation
 Method for Loading Condition 
2.5
.1 Displacement Calculation from Draught Reading 
Hydrostatic data and Cross Curves of stability are ca lculated for even keel (t rim=0). However it may be 
used also for the moderately heeled and trimmed sh ip. The inaccuracy arising is believed to be in 
reasonable limits. 
On condition that the displacement would be determined by reading of draft marks, it becomes necessary 
to calculate an equivalent draft from the measured ones. This is the draft at LCF; it best fits a slightly 
heeled and trimmed ship. 
(1) Read the draught at aft, midship and fore draught marks( Tf0 Ta0 Tm0)
For all m
arks maximum keel thickness of 20 mm is relevant.

FWD #215-0.2 2.3 m aft of F.P.

2.5.2 Trim and stability calculation 
This calculation is made with the attached blank sheets of “Trim and Stability Calculation Sheet 1, 2 and 
3”. 
A
t the first stage the weight, the position of center of gravity and moment distribution are to be calculated 
by “Trim and Stability Calculation Sheet 1 ” a
nd on the next stage trim and stability calculations are 
performed by “Trim and Stability Calculation Sheet 2 and 3”. 
(1) Put t
he weight of cargo, oil, water ballast etc. in to column (1) and put the center of gravity of each
loading weight of length from AP(CGX), breadth from centerline(CGY), and height above base
line(CGZ) into column (2)、(4) and (6) respectively.
CGX, CGY and CGZ corresponding to loading weight for each tank are obtained from “4.2 Tanks
Capa
city Table”.
(2) M
ultiply the value in column (1 ) and (2) ((1) and (4), (1) and (6)), put the results into column (3) ((5)
(7)) as the longitudinal moment, transverse moment and vertical moment of each loading weight.

(3) Put “
Frs. Mom” (the product of specific gravity of liquid and moment of inertia of free surface of each

obtained from “4.2 Tanks Capacity Table”.
(4) The t
otal weight, longitudinal moment, transverse moment, vertical moment and free surface moment
of inertia are to be put into each column in the “Trim and Stability Calculation Sheet 2”
Th
en sum up the columns (1 )’, (3)’, (5)’ and (7)’ re spectively and the results of grand total are to be 
written in the bottom line. These figures mean displacement, longitudinal moment from AP, transverse 
moment from centerline, vertical moment above base li ne and total of free surface effect of liquid in 
tanks respectively.

(10) About free surface correction
For all loading conditions, the initial metacentric  height and the righting lever curve should be
corrected due to free surface of liquids in tanks.
For calculating the free surface effects in tanks cont aining consumable liquids, it should be assumed 
that each type of liquids at leas t one transverse pair or a single center line tank has a free surface and 
the tank or combination of tanks taken into account  should be those where the effects of free surface 
is the greatest.  
Where water ballast tanks are to be filled or discharged during the course of a voyage, the free surface 
effects should be calculated to take account of the most onerous transitory stage relating to such 
operations. It assumes only one ballast tank or pair of tanks is allowed to have a free surface at any 
moment.

3.6. S
tability Limiting Curve 
Please note:
 The following limiting curves calculated  for zero trim: Whereas KG’s of all loading cases 
presented in the booklet is calculated for the corre sponding present trim, because after trim improving 
stability, KG’s of loading cases can be higher than the same value read off the limiting curve. 
Calculated with the following constant values: 
Mean thickness of shell:  0.018 m 
Thickness of keel plate: 0.020 m 
Trim: 0.000 m
Depth (IMO wind criteria)   14.600 m 
Density of sea water:   1.025 t/m 3 
S
tability criteria according to: 
- IMO Resolution A.749(18)
- Minimum area under lever arm curve up to 30 deg. 0.055 mrad
- Minimum area under lever arm curve up to 40 deg. 0.090 mrad
- Minimum area under lever arm curve 30 to 40 deg. 0.030 mrad
- GZ greater than 0.20m at an angle of heel greater than 30 deg.
- Maximum righting arm at an angle greater than 25 deg.
- Initial GM greater than 0.15m
- IMO Resolution A.749(18) (Weather Criterion)
- - Area b / Area a is greater than 1.0
- - Area of bilge keels and bar keel 18.66 m2
- - Gust wind pressure 1.5*0.0514 t/m2 (acc. to reg.)
- -Angle of roll acc. to regulation
- - Heeling angle, steady wind less 16 deg.
- - Heeling angle, steady wind, residual freeboard greater 20%
Explanation of symbols: 
T  D r a u g h t     m  
TR (+) trim by head, (-) trim by stern m 
MINGM Minimum GM in compliance with the criteria   m 
MAXKG Maximum KG in compliance with the criteria   m 
DCRI Name of the determining criterion
